RT entries never take `anira_error`: they return an `anira_status` (or a value: a latency, a plan index), log through the RT queue (`ANIRA_LOG_RT_*`, `src/utils/Logger.h:189-215`), and record the last failure in a per-handler relaxed atomic readable through `anira_handler_rt_error(h)` `[thread-safe] [callback-safe] ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` -- a worklet learns why a Hard entry failed without a message channel. Besides the entries' contract violations it holds `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` after a failed inference (its output zeros) and `ANIRA_ERROR_CAPACITY` after a hop the scheduler dropped because no inference slot was free (its output the miss policy's fill at its stream position); an entry's own `CAPACITY` return is back-pressure and never lands there. Ticket failures will live on the ticket, `anira_handler_ticket_error(h, t, err)`, not on the handler (section 1b). Exceptions never cross: every control-path entry of `src/capi/*.cpp` is `ANIRA_NOEXCEPT` and a function-try-block whose handler is `return anira::capi::translate_exception(err, __func__);` (`src/capi/capi_internal.h:9-27`, `src/capi/error.cpp:147-162`) -- `std::bad_alloc` to `OUT_OF_MEMORY`, `anira::StatusError` to the status it carries (the engine adapters throw their `MODEL_LOAD` and `ENGINE` failures that way), `std::invalid_argument` to `CONFIG`, any other exception to `INTERNAL` with one Error record; a void or destroy entry, which has no channel, writes `report_void_failure(__func__)` and logs instead; an exception that escapes an `ANIRA_NOEXCEPT` entry is a deterministic `std::terminate`. RT bodies are `noexcept` with no handler, because nothing inside them throws by contract. The Wasm build keeps exception catching enabled (`-sNO_DISABLE_EXCEPTION_CATCHING`, `cmake/build-wasm.cmake:76`): Emscripten's default compiles `throw` but disables every `catch`, and the firewall is a `catch`. A caller-owned fixed buffer works identically in a static plugin, in a `dlclose`d DSO and on every Wasm Worker, where a thread-local buffer would be per thread; the frozen 520 bytes let `anira::Result<T>` value-initialise it without an `_INIT` and let TS allocate it with `anira_sizeof(ANIRA_STRUCT_ERROR)` forever.

### Ownership and lifetime

- Create/destroy pairs: `anira_status anira_<x>_create(..., anira_<x>** out, anira_error*)`; `void anira_<x>_destroy(anira_<x>*)`, NULL-safe, a second destroy of the same pointer undefined. Config handles are value-like: a handler copies the pipeline and every config at `anira_handler_create`, and the contract at `anira_handler_prepare` (2.x kept `InferenceConfig&` for life, `v2.3.0:include/anira/InferenceHandler.h:462`), so configs, contracts and pipelines may be destroyed right after the call and TS frees its builders eagerly.
- Descriptor and bytes carriers are refcounted internally: `anira_pipeline_add_processor` copies the processor descriptor into a carrier that the pipeline and every handler created from it share; `anira_custom_engine_create` copies the engine's id and descriptor into the refcounted `anira_custom_engine`, which its handle, every pipeline it is added to, their handlers and the loaded models it serves hold; `add_model_bytes`/`set_model_bytes` wrap the `BORROW`ed bytes in a carrier that every copy shares. `release(user_data)` and `anira_bytes_release_fn` fire exactly once, when the last reference dies, on the thread that drops it -- the caller of `anira_pipeline_destroy`, `anira_handler_destroy`, `anira_custom_engine_destroy`, `anira_model_config_destroy`, or of the `anira_handler_prepare` that drops a loaded model's last handler, all `[main-thread]`; a processor's `release` follows every `unprepare`, an engine's every `unload`. A pipeline destroyed without ever creating a handler releases once; two handlers from one pipeline release once, after the second is destroyed. The engine object is the identity the core's pool shares loaded models by (`src/scheduler/Core.cpp:974-1039`): handlers running one object on an equal model configuration share one load, two objects never share one, even under one id over the same callbacks, and one object under two ids does not exist -- the id is fixed at create.
- Strings in: UTF-8, NUL-terminated, copied by the callee (paths widened internally on Windows). Strings out: `const char*` owned by the object and valid until it is destroyed or mutated, or `(char* buf, size_t cap, size_t* out_len)` with `ANIRA_ERROR_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL`; never on RT paths.
- Model bytes: `ANIRA_BYTES_COPY` or `ANIRA_BYTES_BORROW` plus an optional `release(bytes, ctx)`; borrow is the plugin default (`tanh_add_binary_data` blobs live for the DSO). 2.x borrowed binary blobs silently (`v2.3.0:include/anira/InferenceConfig.h:103-114`); v3 says so.
- Tensor: `release` and `manager_ctx` as in section 1. `release == NULL` means borrowed until the ticket is terminal (Async) or the call returns (Hard). For a user tensor with a non-NULL `release`, one submit is one release call -- exactly once per submitted copy, on an inference thread when the job reaches a terminal state, inside `anira_handler_poll`/`ticket_wait` under Polled delivery, or on the calling thread of `anira_handler_destroy`/`anira_handler_prepare` for jobs still outstanding then; never on the driver thread. Tensors from `anira_handler_allocate_input/allocate_output` are anira-owned pool tensors: anira never invokes their `release`, the producer resubmits the same descriptor over `input_released`, returns it with `anira_handler_free_tensor(h, t)` `[main-thread & prepared]`, and `prepare`/`destroy` free every pool tensor still out. A JS-produced tensor is always borrowed. Through M2 no entry takes a tensor's ownership: the Hard entries and the Static pair borrow until the call returns, anira never calls a `release`, and the holder of a descriptor with a non-NULL `release` calls it once itself (`abi/tensor.h`).
- Sync-token fds (`ANIRA_SYNC_SYNC_FILE_FD`, `ANIRA_SYNC_OPAQUE_FD_SEMAPHORE`): the token owns its fd. `anira_handler_submit` and `anira_handler_bind_output` take `anira_tensor*` (non-const) and move every owning `acquire` token into the slot -- the caller's `acquire.kind` becomes `ANIRA_SYNC_NONE` and `u.fd` becomes `-1` before the call returns, so a later `anira_sync_token_reset` on the caller's copy closes nothing. Tokens read through `ticket_input_released/output_ready` are non-owning views valid until `ticket_release` (no `dup`, so the accessors stay `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`); a caller that needs one longer calls `anira_sync_token_dup(src, out)` `[thread-safe, !audio-thread]`. Owned fds inside a slot are closed by the inference thread when the slot is recycled (deferred close), never by `ticket_release`, which therefore stays `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`; `anira_handler_destroy`/`prepare` close the rest on the calling thread. Other kinds are non-owning.
- Tickets are `uint32_t` values (`slot & 0xffff`, `generation << 16`; `ANIRA_TICKET_INVALID = 0`), never heap objects: `submit` never allocates, `anira_handler_ticket_release` marks the slot for recycling (a still-pending ticket's slot recycles only after the job is terminal; the value is invalid for the caller immediately), `ANIRA_ERROR_CAPACITY` after `lanes * max_in_flight + 8` unreleased tickets, `ANIRA_ERROR_TICKET_STALE` for a recycled slot. A forgotten `ticket_release` surfaces as `ANIRA_ERROR_CAPACITY`; the wrapper's RAII `Ticket` hides it.
- Context: a refcounted handle over the immortal core (`abi/core.h`, `src/scheduler/Core.h:55-62`; one core per copy of anira). `anira_handler_create` adds a reference so the handle's memory outlives the user's `anira_context_destroy`; `anira_context_destroy` drops the user's reference, invalidates the handle for the caller regardless of the count, unregisters its log sink and waits for that sink's in-flight calls, and joins nothing. Thread pool and inference queue are core-owned: the pool exists exactly while any handler in this copy exists; user-driven `anira_inference_thread` objects bind to the core's queue and outlive contexts. `num_threads == 0` keeps meaning "bring your own threads" (`anira_context_config_set_threads`, `abi/config.h:850-863`); `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO = 0xffffffff` is the library default.
- Shutdown, C-visible: `anira_shutdown(void)` (`abi/core.h`; `src/capi/core.cpp:32-46`) runs `Core::shutdown()` (`src/scheduler/Core.cpp:1080-1106`; idempotent, never creates the core) only when no context handle and no handler exist in this copy; otherwise it does nothing and returns `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, so a client of a shared `libanira.so.0` cannot silence another client's sessions. It is meant for static embeddings and is called from `clap_deinit`/`ExitDll` as `examples/clap-audio-plugin/anira-clap-demo-pluginentry.cpp:48-56` does. `anira_release_core_if_idle()` never blocks (on Wasm it also requires that no inference loop still runs) and `anira_has_core()` stay. The unload rule: the ELF/Mach-O library-unload hook (`Core.cpp:380-398`) stays as a backstop for hosts that skipped `anira_handler_destroy`, but v3 runs host code (a custom engine's `process` and `reset`, the processor's `before_inference`/`after_inference`, and at M3 `on_complete` and tensor `release`) on pool threads, so a host that installs a custom processor or engine must destroy its handlers before its DSO is unloaded. With an inference in flight the hook tells the pool to stop and waits up to two seconds; one still running then can never finish under the loader lock, so the hook writes the rule to stderr and aborts instead of hanging. At process exit the core's exit handler, registered when the core is first put to work, stops the pool and waits up to two seconds for the inferences in flight before the exit handlers registered ahead of it run, the static state an engine's runtime built at its first load among them (`Core.cpp:234-267`, `:354-362`), and the hook then joins without a bound. Both run on the thread that called `exit()`, and the core's lifecycle lock knows its holder (`detail::LifecycleMutex::lock_unless_held`, `Core.cpp:49-89`), so an `exit()` from inside an engine's init or load, which run under that lock in `anira_handler_prepare`, ends with its own status instead of deadlocking. Windows keeps the non-joining `UnloadGuard` (`Core.cpp:400-413`); `anira_context_destroy`, `anira_handler_destroy` and `anira_shutdown` are `[main-thread & !loader-lock]`. User-managed `anira_inference_thread` objects must be stopped before unload; on Wasm `anira_inference_thread_destroy` additionally requires `has_exited`.

### Callbacks

`void* user_data` is the third member of every descriptor, `{struct_size, abi_version, user_data, ...}` (`anira_processor_desc`, `anira_engine_desc`, `anira_log_desc`), and travels in every callback beside the per-call arguments: a per-chunk callback is `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`, an unprepare or unload `(prepared, user_data)`, a callback that hands a pointer back ends with its out-parameter (a processor's `prepare(info, user_data, &prepared)`, an engine's `load(info, user_data, &loaded)`, `prepare(info, loaded, user_data, &prepared)` and `query(info, user_data, &available)`), and `anira_miss_fn` and `anira_log_fn` end with it; `anira_tensor_release_proc(anira_tensor*)` takes none and reads the tensor's `manager_ctx`, `anira_bytes_release_fn(bytes, ctx)` its `ctx`. One slot suffices for both wrappers (the C++ wrapper stores a heap control block, the JS bridge a registry key) and its offset survives every tail growth. Every callback fires exactly once per event; re-entrant `submit` from `on_complete` is forbidden in v1. A typedef carries `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` where its slot is RT by contract -- `anira_miss_fn` (`abi/config.h:608-613`), and `anira_job_complete_fn` with `abi/ticket.h` -- so `-Wfunction-effects` flags a blocking body in the consumer's own build; the processor and engine typedefs carry none, because their real-time promise is the descriptor's flags (`ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST`/`_HOOKS`, checked against the placement at `anira_handler_prepare`, and `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_REALTIME_SAFE`).

| callback | thread tag | RT class | descriptor |
|---|---|---|---|
| processor `pre_process`, `post_process`, `reset` (`anira_processor_fn`, `anira_processor_reset_fn`: `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`) | `[driver-thread \| inference-thread]`: the driving thread under Hard (the driver thread, or the thread inside a `_wait` twin), an inference thread under Async; `reset` before the first chunk of a new stream | the processor's promise `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST`, which `anira_handler_prepare` requires of a filled slot under Hard | `anira_processor_desc` |
| processor `before_inference`, `after_inference` | `[driver-thread \| inference-thread]`: an inference thread, around the engine call and its edges | the processor's promise `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_HOOKS` | `anira_processor_desc` |
| processor `prepare(info, user_data, &prepared)`, `unprepare`, `init`, `release` | `[main-thread]`: the caller of `anira_handler_prepare` (`init` once per registration, at its first prepare; `unprepare` at the next prepare or the destroy); `release` on the caller of the last destroy | may allocate; `prepare` may call the `[callback-safe]` entries, the handler getters and the two Static entries; no lock-taking entry | `anira_processor_desc` |
| custom engine `process(ctx, prepared, user_data)`, `reset` | `[inference-thread]` | may block (engine call), must not allocate per call; `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_REALTIME_SAFE` is the engine's promise | `anira_engine_desc` |
| custom engine `init`, `load(info, user_data, &loaded)`, `unload`, `prepare(info, loaded, user_data, &prepared)`, `unprepare`, `query`, `release` | `[main-thread]`: `init` and `load` inside `anira_handler_prepare`, under the core's lifecycle lock; `unload` on the prepare or destroy that drops the loaded model's last handler; `query` at `anira_handler_create` and the capability calls; `release` after every `unload` | may allocate; must not call lock-taking entries | `anira_engine_desc` |
| `anira_miss_fn(handler, inputs, num_inputs, outputs, num_outputs, user_data)` | `[driver-thread]`: a missed block under `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK` | `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` in the typedef | `anira_contract_hard_set_miss_fn` |
| `on_complete` (M3) | `[inference-thread]` under `ANIRA_DELIVERY_IMMEDIATE`; the caller of `anira_handler_poll`/`ticket_wait` under `POLLED` | `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` | `anira_job_options_set_on_complete` |
| log sink `anira_log_fn(record, user_data)` | `[thread-safe, !audio-thread]`: the caller of every `[main-thread]` entry (sync records), the caller of `anira_context_destroy`/`anira_shutdown` (final flush), the drain for RT records (`[drain-thread]`); never the driver thread; may run with anira's lifecycle lock held | not RT, may allocate, must not call anira | `anira_log_desc` |
| tensor `release` (`anira_tensor_release_proc(anira_tensor*)`, reads `manager_ctx`), model-bytes `release(bytes, ctx)` | `[thread-safe, !audio-thread]`: an inference thread or the caller of `poll`/`ticket_wait`/`prepare`/`destroy` (M3; through M2 anira takes no tensor's ownership and never calls it) / `[main-thread]`, on the thread that drops the last carrier | not RT | on the tensor / the bytes carrier |

A phase callback that returns anything but `ANIRA_OK` fails its chunk: the status goes into `rt_error` with one latched record naming the phase, and the chunk delivers zeros at its stream position (v2's `complete_with_zeros`, `v2.3.0:src/scheduler/SessionElement.cpp:236-248`). A failed `pre_process` is not submitted to an engine; a failed `before_inference` or `after_inference` skips the rest and zeroes the model outputs; after a failed `post_process` anira tops every Streamed output ring up to the chunk's hop with zeros, because a ring cannot take a push back. anira counts the hops: after `pre_process` every Streamed input ring must have given up exactly its hop per channel, after `post_process` every Streamed output ring must have gained exactly its hop; a shortfall is repaired (the missing elements discarded from an input ring, an output ring topped up with zeros), an excess cannot be undone, and either records `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` (`abi/processor.h`). A failed engine `process` records `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE`: anira zeroes the outputs, the chunk delivers zeros and a State pair keeps its last good value (`abi/engine.h`). Under Async a failed chunk marks its job `ANIRA_TICKET_FAILED` (M3).

`[callback-safe]` is an additive property: from inside any callback a host may call exactly the entries so tagged and nothing else -- the thirteen `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` tensor factories `anira_tensor_init_*` (nine promised, four draft; `anira_tensor_init_dlpack` is `[main-thread]`), `anira_tensor_data_f32`, `anira_tensor_data`, `anira_tensor_plane`, `anira_tensor_num_elements`, `anira_tensor_extent`, `anira_sizeof`, the ten `anira_ring_*` accessors, the processor-context accessors (`anira_processor_input_role`/`_output_role`, `_input_ring`/`_output_ring`, `_input_tensor`/`_output_tensor`, `anira_processor_engine`, `anira_processor_provider`), `anira_processor_default_pre_process`/`_post_process`, `anira_log_rt`, `anira_now_ms`/`anira_now_ns` and `anira_handler_rt_error` (the 43 functions `abi/anira.yml` marks `callback_safe`); the ticket accessors join at M3 (`anira_handler_ticket_status`, `ticket_input_released`, `ticket_output_ready`, `ticket_error`, `ticket_user_data`, `ticket_release`). A processor's or an engine's `prepare` may additionally use the handler getters and the two Static entries. The reason is a real lock: an engine's `init` and `load` run inside `anira_handler_prepare` under the core's lifecycle lock (`detail::LifecycleMutex`, `src/scheduler/Core.cpp:49-89`), and sync log records are dispatched while that non-recursive lock is held, so an `init`, `load` or log callback that calls `anira_context_create`/`destroy`, `anira_handler_create`/`destroy`/`prepare`, `anira_inference_thread_create` or `anira_shutdown` deadlocks. `ticket_release` from `on_complete` will be legal (the slot recycles after the callback returns); `submit` is not. On Wasm JS supplies no function pointers; `anira_em.h` ships trampolines (`anira_em_processor_desc_init`, `anira_em_engine_desc_init`, `anira_em_set_log_hook`, and `anira_em_job_options_set_on_complete_js` at M3) that fill the same slots with C functions compiled into anira (section 6b); there is none for `anira_miss_fn`.

### Fixed-width types and pinned values

Every enum is a C `enum` with explicit values and a `_FORCE32 = 0x7fffffff` terminator; enums appear as the enum type in parameters and as `uint32_t` fields in structs. `typedef uint32_t anira_bool;`. `anira_dtype` is a packed `uint32_t` = `code | bits << 8 | lanes << 16` (`ANIRA_MAKE_DTYPE`, `ANIRA_DTYPE_F32` and the other constants), whose little-endian bytes are exactly DLPack's `DLDataType {uint8 code; uint8 bits; uint16 lanes}` -- a 4-byte struct is passed `byval` through a pointer on wasm32, a `uint32_t` is not, and `anira_tensor_init_dlpack` packs the DLPack type with `ANIRA_MAKE_DTYPE`. `anira_ticket` is `uint32_t`. Indices, slots, counts of tensors and channels, and sentinels are `uint32_t` (`ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO`, `ANIRA_INSTANCES_AUTO` and `ANIRA_VARIANT_ANY` = `0xffffffff`, `ANIRA_TICKET_INVALID = 0`; the anchor is named by its canonical name, not by a sentinel); sample and element counts are `size_t` (the delivered counts of the Hard entries; a JS `number` on wasm32); durations, deadlines and timeouts are `double` milliseconds (`ANIRA_WAIT_FOREVER = -1.0`, `ANIRA_WAIT_CONTRACT = -2.0`). "Hot" is every `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` declaration: no `int64_t`/`uint64_t` parameter or return there; the allowlist is `anira_now_ns` and the five GPU factories `anira_tensor_init_vulkan`/`_init_opaque_fd`/`_init_wgpu_buffer`/`_init_dmabuf` and the draft `_init_iosurface`, whose parameters are vendor handles, byte offsets and sizes at their wire width and which JS reaches through `BigInt` arguments only, never on its RT path (`WIDE_INT_ALLOWLIST`, `tools/abi/gen.py:85-92`); `int64_t` otherwise appears only in config-time shape/window/extent setters (`BigInt` at config time only) and in the `anira_tensor` fields, which JS reads through `layout.ts`. Any `uint32_t`/`size_t` value >= 2^31 returned to JS arrives as a negative i32 and needs `>>> 0`; the generated `web/src/abi/bindings.ts` applies it to every unsigned and pointer return (`ts_ret_conv`, `tools/abi/gen.py:1605-1612`), so no caller can forget it.

Every enum value is pinned independently of `USE_*` (2.x's `enum InferenceBackend` shifted with the build, `v2.3.0:include/anira/utils/InferenceBackend.h:32-110`, and its web wrapper exported `get_inference_backend_onnx()` to cope, `v2.3.0:src/emscripten-wrappers/InferenceConfig.cpp:18`). Engine and execution provider are two independent enums, never one packed id: a provider name means the same thing across engines -- CUDA is CUDA whether ONNX Runtime or LibTorch runs it -- which the packed form hid. Engine values `6..0x7ffffffd` are reserved for later built-in engines, appended after `ANIRA_ENGINE_TFLITE`; a custom engine is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` (`0x7ffffffe`) beside its `engine_id`, the reverse-URI id `anira_custom_engine_create` gives it -- the reservation section 1b already makes for third-party extension kinds -- so anira's short engine names can never collide, and wherever an engine is named the pair rule holds: an id if and only if the value is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM`. Providers follow the same rule, `8..0x7ffffffd` reserved and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` beside a `provider_id`. Domain values `13-63` are reserved for later domains (12 is the reserved `ANIRA_DOMAIN_FRAME`).

```c
typedef enum anira_engine   { ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE = 0, ANIRA_ENGINE_ONNXRUNTIME = 1, ANIRA_ENGINE_EXECUTORCH = 2, ANIRA_ENGINE_LITERT = 3,
    ANIRA_ENGINE_LIBTORCH = 4, ANIRA_ENGINE_TFLITE = 5,
    /* 6 .. 0x7ffffffd reserved for later built-in engines, appended after TFLITE */
    ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM = 0x7ffffffe /* a custom engine, named by its id */,
    ANIRA_ENGINE_FORCE32 = 0x7fffffff } anira_engine;
typedef enum anira_provider { ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE = 0 /* no provider */, ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU = 1 /* the engine's plain CPU path */,
    ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUDA = 2, ANIRA_PROVIDER_WEBGPU = 3, ANIRA_PROVIDER_VULKAN = 4, ANIRA_PROVIDER_DIRECTML = 5,
    ANIRA_PROVIDER_COREML = 6, ANIRA_PROVIDER_XNNPACK = 7,
    /* 8 .. 0x7ffffffd reserved for later providers, appended after XNNPACK */
    ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM = 0x7ffffffe /* a provider named by its id */,
    ANIRA_PROVIDER_FORCE32 = 0x7fffffff } anira_provider;
/* where the pair must travel as one item, a Tier-2 record */
typedef struct anira_backend_id { uint32_t struct_size; uint32_t engine; uint32_t provider; const char* engine_id; const char* provider_id; } anira_backend_id;   /* abi/context.h */
    /* engine_id: the custom engine's id beside ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM, provider_id the custom provider's name beside ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM; NULL otherwise */
```

| `anira_engine` | value | v2 name / JSON string |
|---|---|---|
| `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` | `0` | -- |
| `ANIRA_ENGINE_ONNXRUNTIME` | `1` | `ONNX` / `"onnxruntime"` |
| `ANIRA_ENGINE_EXECUTORCH` | `2` | `EXECUTORCH` / `"executorch"` |
| `ANIRA_ENGINE_LITERT` | `3` | `LITERT` / `"litert"` |
| `ANIRA_ENGINE_LIBTORCH` | `4` | `LIBTORCH` / `"libtorch"` |
| `ANIRA_ENGINE_TFLITE` | `5` | `TFLITE` / `"tflite"` |
| `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` | `0x7ffffffe` | `CUSTOM` (the id `anira.v2.custom`) / the engine's own id |
| `ANIRA_ENGINE_FORCE32` | `0x7fffffff` | -- |

| `anira_provider` | value | JSON word (a model entry's `"provider"`) |
|---|---|---|
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` | `0` | -- (no key: a neutral entry) |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU` | `1` | `"cpu"` |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUDA` | `2` | `"cuda"` |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_WEBGPU` | `3` | `"webgpu"` |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_VULKAN` | `4` | `"vulkan"` |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DIRECTML` | `5` | `"directml"` |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_COREML` | `6` | `"coreml"` |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_XNNPACK` | `7` | `"xnnpack"` |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` | `0x7ffffffe` | the provider's own name |
| `ANIRA_PROVIDER_FORCE32` | `0x7fffffff` | -- |

A v2 `InferenceBackend` names an engine and never a provider, so v2 `ONNX` is `ANIRA_ENGINE_ONNXRUNTIME` with `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU`, `LIBTORCH` is `ANIRA_ENGINE_LIBTORCH` with `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU`, and so on through the five; v2 `CUSTOM` is a custom engine id, `anira.v2.custom`, not a value: the version-2 upgrade names every `CUSTOM` row so, and an engine on the pipeline serves it (`compat/v2.hpp` creates one).

Upper-case v2 strings are accepted on the JSON upgrade path (section 8). Enum values, status codes and the ABI-version packing macros reach TS as `web/src/abi/enums.ts`, generated by `tools/abi/gen.py` from the registry `abi/anira.yml`, the same source those headers are generated from; a page states the packed version its code was written against as `Anira.create({abiVersion})`, which `anira_check_abi` answers (`web/src/Anira.ts:123-132`).

### Thread tags

Every declaration carries one tag (the dual `[driver-thread | inference-thread]` is one tag of the vocabulary); `[callback-safe]` is added where it applies. The vocabulary is CLAP's (`clap/include/clap/plugin.h:41-110`, `ext/thread-check.h:37-41`: "functions marked with [audio-thread] ARE NOT CONCURRENT"), with CLAP's `[audio-thread]` renamed `[driver-thread]` because anira drives frames as well as samples and the tag never named the medium, extended by the roles anira has and CLAP does not:

- `[main-thread]` -- control path, externally serialised per object, may allocate and block; `[main-thread & !prepared]`, `[main-thread & prepared]`, `[main-thread & !processing]` -- state-qualified; `[main-thread & !loader-lock]` -- never from `DllMain`/`dlclose` paths.
- `[driver-thread]` -- the thread that drives a Hard contract: the host's real-time callback, whatever the medium -- an audio device callback, a video frame callback, a render loop. `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, not concurrent per handler: two driver threads may drive two handlers of one context, never one handler. "Not concurrent" holds per handler, not per context. The tag names the role, not the medium, which is why `pre_process`, tagged `[driver-thread | inference-thread]`, runs as the one under Hard and as `[inference-thread]` under Async.
- `[inference-thread]` -- anira's pool or a user `anira_inference_thread`.
- `[driver-thread | inference-thread]` -- the processor's: its phase and reset callbacks run on the driving thread under Hard (`pre_process`, `post_process`, `reset`) and on an inference thread for `before_inference`/`after_inference` and under Async, and so do the context accessors and default bodies they call; anira's entries under the tag are `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, the callback typedefs carry no attribute (the processor's flags are the promise).
- `[thread-safe]` -- any thread, concurrently; `[thread-safe, !audio-thread]` -- any thread except the driver thread (fd-closing token calls, control-path logging, `anira_drain_log`, the log sink and tensor-release callbacks).
- `[callback-safe]` (`[cs]`) -- additive: also legal from inside any anira callback.
- `[drain-thread]` -- the log sink's thread for RT records; a real-time record may also be delivered on the thread of a failing `[main-thread]` entry, which drains the queue before returning a negative status.
- `[any-thread, blocking]` -- may wait (the `_wait` twins, `anira_handler_ticket_wait`); on Wasm every wait spins, so inside the worklet calling one is a host policy decision.

### What anira borrows from CLAP, and what it does not

Applied: per-slot thread tags on every declaration (the vocabulary above, with `[inference-thread]`, `[drain-thread]`, `[callback-safe]`, `[any-thread, blocking]` and the state qualifiers added because anira has more thread roles than CLAP's two); version-first host-provided vtables with the user pointer second (`clap_host.clap_version`/`host_data`, `clap_plugin.plugin_data`) -- `anira_processor_desc`, `anira_engine_desc`, `anira_log_desc` are `{struct_size, abi_version, user_data, ...}`, with one packed `uint32_t` instead of `{major, minor, revision}` because every other version in the header is packed and TS writes one field; the compat macros (`CLAP_ABI` `__cdecl` on 32-bit Windows, one header for C and C++) as `ANIRA_CALL`, `ANIRA_INIT`, the `extern "C"` guards and the C11-plus-C++17 compile test; the `init`/`deinit` refcount rule of `clap_entry`, applied per DSO as CLAP means it, while `anira_shutdown` additionally refuses while any context or handler exists in the copy -- a rule CLAP does not need because a CLAP DSO is never shared, and the one that protects a shared `libanira.so.0` between two clients; string ids with a revision and a draft folder (`conventions/extension-id.md`, `clap.h` vs `all.h`) -- extension kinds are strings with a `version` field in `anira_ext_header`, the registry key is `(kind, version)` with the revision as a field rather than a `/REV` suffix because the kind doubles as the JSON key, `abi/draft/` plus `anira_all.h` carry unmeasured surfaces with their own baselines, promotion never renames, no `_COMPAT` ids ever, reverse-URI third-party kinds reserved for after 3.0; size-first records for what the host enumerates (`events.h`) -- `anira_edge_info`, `anira_plan_slot`, `anira_plan_ext` are `struct_size`-first, walked by stride and count, rows with pointers valid for the call only, whereas `anira_log_record` and `anira_error` are frozen Tier-1 PODs because one is read per drained record and the other is value-initialised by `Result<T>`; allocation confined to activation (`plugin.h:61`) restated as no allocation after `prepare` on any `[driver-thread]`/`[inference-thread]` path -- `anira_handler_prepare` loads the models, `allocate_*` (M3) allocates by name, `anira_context_create` probes, all `[main-thread]`, and `get_latency` is valid from `prepare` on; render mode as contract kind (`ext/render.h`) -- `anira_contract_kind` at `prepare` is CLAP's `set(REALTIME | OFFLINE)`, `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` its refusal; log severities (`ext/log.h` `HOST_MISBEHAVING`/`PLUGIN_MISBEHAVING`) with a deviation -- the four v2 levels stay and a contract violation is a record with `ANIRA_LOG_RECORD_CONTRACT_VIOLATION` set, so "most verbose wins" ordering is unchanged; `clap_id`/`CLAP_INVALID_ID` and caller-owned text buffers (`ext/params.h`) as `uint32_t` indices, `ANIRA_TICKET_INVALID = 0` and `(buf, cap, *out_len)`.

Deliberately not borrowed: string-keyed `get_extension` near the driver thread (everything string-keyed resolves at `prepare`; every entry point is linked in every build, and what a build lacks answers `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` or is absent from `anira_enabled_engines`); the plugin-side vtable as the library boundary (entry points are flat functions; vtables exist only for host-provided services and are not callable from JS otherwise); `bool` as the only error channel; bundling the process call into one struct (the hot path is frozen at one `const anira_tensor*` per slot); host-pumped main-thread machinery (`request_callback`, timer-support, posix-fd-support -- anira has no event loop, only `anira_handler_poll`); fixed 256-byte name arrays in hot structs (names live on `anira_tensor_spec` only); `clap_version_is_compatible = major >= 1` (anira majors may break; `anira_check_abi` tests major equality); blocking `thread-pool.request_exec`, a fork-join inside `process()` (anira's threads are long-lived `anira_inference_thread` loops; a host-provided thread-provider vtable is deferred); the export-only `CLAP_EXPORT` (anira needs import/export and an Emscripten branch); convention-only stability (CLAP ships no checker; anira ships six gates, five running through the alphas and the abidiff from the freeze).

### Gates

Six gates own the contract, each covering one failure mode on every platform; five run through the alphas and the sixth comes with the freeze (`docs/sphinx/contributing.rst`, "The ABI gates"; section 11 lists the schedule): `anira_symbol_baseline` diffs the shared library's real export table against `abi/symbols-<major>.txt` ∪ `abi/symbols-draft.txt`, both lists alike -- a missing promised or draft name fails, and so does any other exported name -- with `anira_abi_link` as the presence check of the static legs, the `-sEXPORTED_FUNCTIONS` link of AniraWeb as that of WebAssembly, and `anira_exports` (`test/contracts/unload`) forbidding every engine runtime and every private dependency in the library's and a plugin-shaped module's table (gate 1); `anira_abi_diff` will run libabigail against the last tag's baseline, once over the whole header set and once over the Tier-1 headers alone (gate 2, from the freeze); `anira_abi_layout` prints every Tier-1 `sizeof`/`offsetof` and enum width natively and under node and diffs it against `abi/layout-<major>.txt` (gate 3); `anira_header_c11`, `anira_header_cxx17`, `anira_header_cxx20`, `anira_header_compat`, `anira_header_compat_with_hpp` and the strict `anira_header_isolation` compile every public header alone and the umbrellas together as C11 and C++17 with no anira define, `anira.hpp` and the shim as C++20, and prove that nothing private (`src/`, an engine, tanh-lib, the queue, the JSON library, gtest, benchmark) resolves through `anira::anira`'s usage requirements, on every preset (gate 4); `InstallConsumer` (`test/contracts/install/`) and `build_install` link the installed package shared and static: `consumer` (`anira.hpp`), `consumer_c` (`main_c.c`: pure C11, the raw-C sketch of section 9 over an engine made with `anira_custom_engine_create` and `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_NEEDS_NO_MODEL`), `consumer_compat`, `consumer_engine`, `consumer_tanh_first` and a plugin-shaped module that exports no engine symbol and no `anira_*` entry (gate 5); the RTSan leg runs the whole suite under RealtimeSanitizer (`desktop-tests-rtsan`, the unload harness excepted), with `-Werror=function-effects` on the consumer-shaped targets (`anira_abi_rt_contract`, `anira_header_c11`, `anira_abi_link`, `consumer_c`, `anira_header_cxx20`, `anira_header_compat`) where the compiler has the analysis (gate 6). The presence check is an anira-local script (`cmake/abi-symbols.cmake`), and the abidiff will be one, until a tanh-tooling tag after v3.0.0 carries them.

---

## 6b. The boundary: tanh-lib, third parties, and the WebAssembly build

One copy of anira is one self-contained world: hidden visibility, `-fno-gnu-unique`, a private thread pool, a private logger, and tanh-lib's objects inside `libanira`. tanh-lib, concurrentqueue and nlohmann_json are PRIVATE and invisible on every platform -- no public header includes them, logging crosses as a C callback, and tanh-lib gets no C ABI of its own. The WebAssembly build is the fifth static-embedding target, with a JavaScript host instead of a plugin host; nothing in it is a second ABI. `anira_exports` forbids `thl`, `moodycamel` and `nlohmann` in the export table (`test/contracts/unload/CMakeLists.txt:115-119`), `anira_header_isolation` proves no third-party include is reachable through `anira::anira`, and `consumer_tanh_first` proves the two copies of tanh-lib stay apart where the build separates them: a shared libanira and the prelinked static ELF one. A plain static archive (Mach-O, PE) shares one copy with a host's tanh-lib, the case the two-copies paragraph below states (section 6a).

### tanh-lib: absorbed, private, no C ABI

Every place the 2.x headers leaked tanh-lib or another dependency, cited at `v2.3.0`, and how M2 closed it:

| leak (v2.3.0) | closed by |
|---|---|
| `v2.3.0:include/anira/system/Exports.h:30` includes `<tanh/core/ExportMacros.h>` | `Exports.h`, `AniraExports.h` and `AniraWinExports.h` are deleted; the export header is the generated, self-contained `abi/export.h`, and no C++ class or function carries `ANIRA_API` (section 6a) |
| `v2.3.0:include/anira/utils/Logger.h:4` and `v2.3.0:include/anira/scheduler/Context.h:4,542` include `<tanh/core/Logger.h>`; `v2.3.0:include/anira/utils/Logger.h:112` exports `rt_log_queue_slot()` returning `std::atomic<thl::Logger::rt::Queue*>&` | both private (`src/utils/Logger.h`, `src/scheduler/Core.h`), `rt_log_queue_slot()` exported no more; logging crosses as `anira_log_desc` in and `anira_log_record` out (section 4); the slot stays constant-initialised per copy (`src/utils/Logger.cpp:9-13`) |
| `v2.3.0:include/anira/scheduler/InferenceThread.h:9,226` holds a `thl::core::Thread` member; `v2.3.0:include/anira/system/HighPriorityThread.h:4` | `src/scheduler/InferenceThread.h` private, the opaque `anira_inference_thread` of `abi/thread.h` its C face; `HighPriorityThread` deleted |
| `v2.3.0:include/anira/utils/Buffer.h:4,29`, `v2.3.0:include/anira/utils/RingBuffer.h:4,39-43`, `v2.3.0:include/anira/utils/MemoryBlock.h:4,19` are aliases over `thl::core` templates | `Buffer.h` and `MemoryBlock.h` deleted, `RingBuffer.h` private (`src/utils/RingBuffer.h`, the storage of `anira_ring`); consumers see `anira_ring*` and `anira_tensor`, v2 subclasses the `compat/v2.hpp` views (section 7) |
| `v2.3.0:include/anira/scheduler/SessionElement.h:4,6` includes `<concurrentqueue.h>` and `<blockingconcurrentqueue.h>` and names `moodycamel::*` (`:264, 297-302`); `v2.3.0:include/anira/utils/Semaphore.h:34` | private (`src/scheduler/SessionElement.h`, `src/utils/Semaphore.h`) |
| `v2.3.0:include/anira/utils/JsonConfigLoader.h:8` includes `<nlohmann/json.hpp>` | deleted; JSON crosses as UTF-8 text (section 8), parsed inside the library (`src/capi/json.cpp`), and `compat/v2.hpp`'s `JsonConfigLoader` sits over the `_from_json` entries |
| `v2.3.0:include/anira/benchmark/ProcessBlockFixture.h:4,8` includes `<benchmark/benchmark.h>` and the v2 umbrella; `v2.3.0:cmake/benchmark-src.cmake:16` and `v2.3.0:cmake/test-deps.cmake:7` link `benchmark`/`gtest_main` PUBLIC; `v2.3.0:cmake/install.cmake:39` installs the whole `include/anira` tree; `v2.3.0:Config.cmake.in:30,34` looks up GTest and benchmark | the fixture is `examples/benchmark/fixture/` over `anira.hpp` (`anira_benchmark_fixture`, static, not installed); `benchmark` and `gtest_main` are never linked into `anira` (every gtest binary links `gtest_main` itself, `cmake/test-deps.cmake`); the install is the explicit file list below; `Config.cmake.in` lost both lookups |
| `v2.3.0:CMakeLists.txt:331` links `concurrentqueue nlohmann_json::nlohmann_json tanh::Core` PUBLIC; `v2.3.0:Config.cmake.in:20-27` runs `find_package(concurrentqueue)`, `find_package(nlohmann_json)`, `find_dependency(tanh COMPONENTS Core)`; `v2.3.0:cmake/install.cmake:49` installs all three targets | `anira_objects` links them PUBLIC, for the build alone (`CMakeLists.txt:583`), and `anira` none of them; nothing of them is installed, and `Config.cmake.in` runs `find_dependency(Threads)` alone; `TANH_WITH_INSTALL OFF`, concurrentqueue's own CMake never runs, and nlohmann_json installs nothing as a subproject |
| tanh_core's PUBLIC defines `TANH_VERSION`, `THL_LOG_COMPILED_MAX_LEVEL`, `THL_PLATFORM_*` (`tanh-lib/CMakeLists.txt:151-172` at 7ae7c69) and the PUBLIC `-fsanitize=realtime` of `tanh_add_sanitizer` | they reach `anira_objects` and the white-box test binaries alone; `ANIRA_WITH_RTSAN` re-exports only `-fsanitize=realtime`, to anira's own consumers |
| `TORCH_CXX_FLAGS` PUBLIC (`v2.3.0:cmake/backends.cmake:743-748`), pinning the `std::string` ABI of the public API | PUBLIC on `anira_objects` alone (`CMakeLists.txt:588-595`): no C++ type crosses libanira's boundary, so its consumers do not get it |

Embedding. tanh_core's objects are absorbed into `libanira`. tanh-lib stays a `FetchContent` subtree at its pin (`GIT_TAG 7ae7c690d57806acc56dd4b5ffbba50551913966`, v0.4.0 plus tanh-tooling v0.3.0, `CMakeLists.txt:216-228`), its own CMake runs -- `tanh_apply_symbol_policy(tanh_core EXPORT_PREFIX TANH)` and the `NAMESPACE thl` allowlist -- and the same-tag warning of `modules-version.cmake` stays alive; compiling tanh's translation units directly is rejected, because it would fork tanh-lib's build. anira compiles its own sources once, into the OBJECT library `anira_objects`, which carries every build-time requirement; `anira`, shared or static, is linked from the two object sets alone (a static ELF one from the single prelinked object of both, section 6a). The shape, trimmed from `CMakeLists.txt`:

```cmake
# CMakeLists.txt -- tanh_core built by its own CMake as a hidden static archive, its objects absorbed
set(TANH_WITH_INSTALL OFF)
set(TANH_LOG_COMPILED_MAX_LEVEL 4)               # one compiled log ceiling in every build type (a tanh-lib cache option; plain variable = CMP0077 override)
FetchContent_Declare(tanh-lib GIT_REPOSITORY https://github.com/tanh-lab/tanh-lib.git
    GIT_TAG 7ae7c690d57806acc56dd4b5ffbba50551913966)   # v0.4.0 plus tanh-tooling v0.3.0
set(_anira_build_shared_libs ${BUILD_SHARED_LIBS})
set(BUILD_SHARED_LIBS OFF)                       # scoped: TANH_STATIC PUBLIC, TANH_API empty (cmake/tanh/symbol-policy.cmake:110-117)
FetchContent_MakeAvailable(tanh-lib)
set(BUILD_SHARED_LIBS ${_anira_build_shared_libs})
#   a parent's tanh_core that is not a STATIC_LIBRARY with TANH_STATIC: FATAL_ERROR; a log ceiling other than 4: WARNING
add_library(anira_objects OBJECT)                # every source of anira, once, under the symbol policy
target_link_libraries(anira_objects PUBLIC anira_concurrentqueue nlohmann_json::nlohmann_json tanh::Core)   # include dirs and defines; the white-box tests link the objects
add_library(anira $<TARGET_OBJECTS:anira_objects> $<TARGET_OBJECTS:tanh_core>)   # static ELF: STATIC over anira_prelinked.o (cmake/prelink-archive.cmake)
tanh_set_export_allowlist(anira SYMBOL "anira_*")
if(TANH_OPERATING_SYSTEM STREQUAL "Android")
    target_link_libraries(anira PRIVATE log)     # tanh_core's one PRIVATE system link, repeated
endif()
```

`$<TARGET_OBJECTS:tanh_core>` puts the five objects of `tanh-lib/CMakeLists.txt:130-136` (core.cpp, Dispatcher, Logger, Thread, RCU) into `libanira`, and `anira` links none of the three dependencies, so `install(EXPORT aniraTargets)` never demands `tanh_core` in an export set and no `concurrentqueue` or `nlohmann_json` target is exported or installed. The PUBLIC links of `anira_objects` carry the include directories and defines (`TANH_STATIC`, `THL_PLATFORM_*`) to the objects and to the white-box binaries that link them, and to nothing else. concurrentqueue is fetched without running its own CMake and wrapped in the build-only interface target `anira_concurrentqueue`; it and `nlohmann_json` are header-only and `SYSTEM`. No `--exclude-libs` entry for tanh-lib exists, since no tanh-lib archive is linked; `tanh_hidden_archive_link_items` stays for the engine archives (`cmake/aniraEngineHelpers.cmake:142`). `THL_LOG_COMPILED_MAX_LEVEL` is consumed only inside `Logger.cpp` (`tanh-lib/src/core/Logger.cpp:46-62`) and is `1` in Release by tanh's default (`TANH_LOG_COMPILED_MAX_LEVEL=AUTO`); anira sets the option to `4` before the fetch, so `anira_log_desc.level` means the same thing in every build type and a v2 file's `ANIRA_LOG_WARNING` reaches a Release sink. A project that fetched tanh-lib before anira makes anira's fetch a no-op and its own `tanh_core` the one anira absorbs, so anira checks that target after the fetch (`CMakeLists.txt:240-264`): one that is not a static archive with `TANH_STATIC` among its interface definitions is a `FATAL_ERROR` (a shared `tanh_core` would export `thl` beside anira and bind to a host's copy, and the prelink would keep its default-visibility symbols global), and one whose compiled log ceiling is not 4 is a warning (anira's records below it are compiled out of a Release build, and no context config's level brings them back).

Why this closes the two-copies hazard, and where it does not. anira's translation units see `TANH_STATIC`, so `TANH_API` is empty and every thl definition is hidden under `-fvisibility=hidden`; anira's references to `thl::Logger::set_callback`, `thl::Logger::rt::Queue` or `thl::core::Thread` stay ordinary default-visibility undefined references (the option hides definitions, not the declarations a reference names), which the first definition the link resolves satisfies. A shared libanira (`libanira.so.0` through the 3.0.0 pre-releases, `libanira.so.3` from v3.0.0) resolves them inside itself and exports the `anira_*` entries alone (`tanh_set_export_allowlist`), so nothing of its tanh-lib can bind to a host's or a host's to it, on ELF, Mach-O and PE. A static `libanira.a` is linked into the consumer's image, where hidden separates nothing; on ELF it is one prelinked object (`cmake/prelink-archive.cmake`) in which those references are resolved and every hidden definition but `anira_*` is local, so the copies stay apart there too, except tanh-lib's header-only code, whose weak COMDAT instantiations the final link merges with a host's of the same signature (one tanh-lib version assumed). A plain static archive (Mach-O, PE, a multi-config generator, a toolchain without readelf and objcopy) keeps its objects: one copy serves both, the one the link order puts first (with a host's `tanh::Core` first anira binds to the host's copy, an ABI mismatch when the versions differ); `docs/sphinx/troubleshooting.rst` documents it, and a host that uses tanh-lib links the shared anira there. `test/contracts/install/consumer_tanh_first` -- a consumer that links a shared `tanh::Core` first and `anira::anira` second and checks that neither logger sees the other's records -- runs on both ELF install legs and the shared Mach-O one. Two copies of tanh_core in one process (two plugins, or a plugin beside a host that uses tanh-lib) each own their `g_runtime_level`, `state()`, `default_queue()`, `atexit` hook and `rcu_thread_state()`; with the objects absorbed and hidden nothing of either copy is in a dynamic symbol table and, beside a shared or a prelinked static libanira, nothing can bind across. The only visible effect is two `"anira-log"` drain threads, which is accepted: merging them would require tanh-lib to become a shared dependency again, the situation being left.

Logging. anira keeps a private `thl::Logger` -- the copy inside `libanira` -- and installs one trampoline on it (`thl::Logger::set_callback`, installed while any sink exists, `src/utils/LogSinks.cpp`) that fans out to a per-copy sink registry with one entry `{callback, user_data, level, in_flight}` per context that set a sink; `anira_context_destroy` unregisters its entry and blocks until the entry's in-flight count is zero, because thl dispatches a callback from a copy, outside its mutex (`tanh-lib/src/core/Logger.cpp:520-531, 561-565` at 7ae7c69) and a clear alone would not suffice. An application that links tanh-lib has a second, invisible logger that anira never touches. `anira_log_record` is a projection of `thl::Logger::LogRecord`; the level mapping, the RT queue, the drain thread and the platform-sink flag are stated once, in section 4.

`Thread`, `RCU`, `Dispatcher` and `LockFreeQueue` are internal. `thl::RCU<T>` (namespace `thl`, not `thl::core`) is M2b's selection primitive natively (PR V; M5's before the roadmap revision of 2026-10-29), and anira instantiates it nowhere yet: it keys reader identity on a `static thread_local` (`tanh-lib/src/core/RCU.cpp:26-29`). Until then selection is one relaxed atomic, the dense plan index `SessionElement::select_plan` stores (`src/scheduler/SessionElement.cpp:71-75`). The two `thread_local` objects tanh_core carries (`Logger.cpp:466`, the callback-dispatch depth, and `RCU.cpp:27`) are per thread on every target: since PR 11 Emscripten gives every Wasm Worker and the worklet a stack and a thread-local block of their own (`anira/anira_em.h`).

Pin rule. anira's tanh-lib pin may move freely (`7ae7c69` today: v0.4.0 plus tanh-tooling v0.3.0); anira and the fetched tanh-lib must carry the same tanh-tooling release in `cmake/tanh/` (v0.3.0 today; `cmake/tanh/modules-version.cmake:14-28` records it and warns on a mismatch), so a tooling bump is a two-repository PR pair. Every CMake change of this section uses the modules that exist; what they lack is an anira-local script under `anira/cmake/` (`prelink-archive.cmake`, `public-headers.cmake`) until the tanh-tooling tag after v3.0.0 that both repositories re-pin together (section 11). tanh-lib gets no C ABI: nothing of it is a binary contract of anira's, and a tanh-lab plugin that wants `thl` links tanh-lib itself.

The installed package, as an explicit file list:

| path | content |
|---|---|
| `include/anira/{anira.h, anira_all.h, anira_em.h, anira.hpp, compat/v2.hpp}`, `include/anira/abi/*.h`, `include/anira/abi/draft/*.h` | the header set (section 6a); nothing else under `include/anira` |
| `lib/libanira.so.0.2` + `libanira.so.0` + namelink, `lib/libanira.0.dylib`, `lib/libanira.a` (one prelinked object on ELF, a plain archive elsewhere); `bin/anira-0.dll` + `lib/anira-0.lib`, a static PE library `lib/anira.lib` | the library: `VERSION` and `MACHO_*` the ABI pair of `cmake/build-info.cmake`, `SOVERSION` its major, `OUTPUT_NAME anira-<major>` for a PE DLL (`CMakeLists.txt:402-415`); the major is 0 through the 3.0.0 pre-releases and 3 from v3.0.0 |
| `include/anira-engines/<engine>/`, the engine libraries | the payload of the `anira::<engine>` targets; `anira::anira` reaches none of it, and a consumer whose own code calls an engine links `anira::<engine>` |
| `lib/cmake/anira/{aniraConfig.cmake, aniraConfigVersion.cmake, aniraTargets.cmake, aniraEngineTargets.cmake, aniraEngineHelpers.cmake, tanh/{modules-version,platform,symbol-policy}.cmake}` | `aniraConfig.cmake` runs `find_dependency(Threads)` alone and defines the engine targets before `aniraTargets.cmake`; `SameMajorVersion` of the project version |
| -- | no `include/tanh`, no `libtanh_core`, no concurrentqueue, no nlohmann, no benchmark or gtest headers |

### WebAssembly: the C ABI with a JavaScript host

Deleted at M2: `src/emscripten-wrappers/` -- eleven files, 1865 lines, 226 `EMSCRIPTEN_KEEPALIVE` entry points with `uintptr_t` handles, `int` enums, 43 `vector_*` shims (48 `EMSCRIPTEN_KEEPALIVE` entries in its `utils/Vectors.cpp`, five of them `buffer_f_*`) and no error path -- together with the `anira_wasm_wrappers` archive and the `src/emscripten-wrappers/` exclusion in `clang_tidy.yml`. The C ABI implementation in `src/capi/` is compiled into `libanira.a` on every target, Emscripten included; the only Emscripten-specific code is `src/platform/emscripten/`, compiled on the Wasm binary format alone (`CMakeLists.txt:489-513`): `em_hooks.cpp` (the `EM_JS` thunks into the scope registries, the processor and engine trampolines, the JS log hook, the context's worker module), `em_worker.cpp` (an inference thread as a Wasm Worker: its region, the boot that imports the worker module, the reaper of a cancelled boot), `em_audio.cpp` (the Wasm Audio Worklet node), `em_console.cpp` (the platform sink, the console by level) and `em_runtime.cpp` (the runtime that never exits). JavaScript reaches every entry as `Module._anira_<x>`, typed by the generated `web/src/abi/bindings.ts`.

Link line. `cmake/build-wasm.cmake` links the `--no-entry` `AniraWeb` module from `libanira.a` alone, without a whole-archive link or `EXPORT_KEEPALIVE`, against the generated export list:

```cmake
add_executable(AniraWeb "${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/aniraweb_stub.cpp")
target_link_libraries(AniraWeb PRIVATE anira::anira)                # no wrapper archive, no --whole-archive
#   --no-entry -pthread -sWASM_WORKERS=1 -sAUDIO_WORKLET=1            # Emscripten's hybrid mode: Wasm Workers and the Wasm Audio Worklet, one shared memory
#   -sINCOMING_MODULE_JS_API=wasmMemory,wasmBinary,locateFile,instantiateWasm,print,printErr -sEXIT_RUNTIME=0
#   --emit-tsd=web/wasm/AniraWeb.d.ts                                 # MainModule typing: _malloc/_free/HEAP* and every _anira_* signature
#   -sIMPORTED_MEMORY=1 -sSHARED_MEMORY=1 -sINITIAL_MEMORY=268435456 -sALLOW_MEMORY_GROWTH=1 -sMAXIMUM_MEMORY=2147483648 -sMALLOC=emmalloc
#   -sEXPORT_ES6=1 -sMODULARIZE=1 -sENVIRONMENT=web,worker,worklet -sSTACK_SIZE=33554432 -sSTACK_OVERFLOW_CHECK=0
#   -sASSERTIONS=0                                                    # 1 (with -O0 -gsource-map) outside Release: libwebaudio.js asserts a window.AudioContext and refuses an OfflineAudioContext
#   -sNO_DISABLE_EXCEPTION_CATCHING                                   # the exception firewall's catch needs it (section 6a)
#   -sEXPORTED_FUNCTIONS=@web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt                # _malloc, _free and every promised, draft and Emscripten entry point of abi/anira.yml
#   -sEXPORTED_RUNTIME_METHODS=UTF8ToString,stringToUTF8OnStack,lengthBytesUTF8,HEAPU8,HEAP32,HEAPU32,HEAPF32,HEAPF64,HEAP64,HEAPU64,
#                              stackSave,stackRestore,stackAlloc,emscriptenRegisterAudioObject,emscriptenGetAudioObject,wasmMemory
#   JOB_POOL_LINK anira_wasm_link                                     # depth 2: one link peaks at about 4.9 GB
#   POST_BUILD cmake/wasm-glue-rewrite.cmake                          # the two sites of the glue Vite's dev server trips on
```

The export list alone pulls the archive members, and an undefined name fails the link, which is the Wasm presence gate; no `EMSCRIPTEN_KEEPALIVE` and no `used` attribute exist in the header. The list is the committed, generated `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt`, which `gen.py --check` keeps in step with the registry and the link reads itself (`LINK_DEPENDS`). Exception catching stays on because Emscripten's default compiles `throw` and disables every `catch`, including the firewall's; `-fwasm-exceptions` is a size and speed choice once the ORT wasm archive in the backends release is built with it. The memory is imported, shared and growing: `Anira.create` makes it, 256 MiB to begin with and 2 GiB at most, which keeps every heap address below 2^31; it never shrinks, and the package re-derives its views after a growth (`heap(module)` of `web/src/abi/bindings.ts`). Every link against anira on Wasm also carries `-sABORTING_MALLOC=0`, so that out of memory is `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` and not an abort, and the `--undefined=` anchors of the console sink and of the runtime that never exits (`CMakeLists.txt:489-513`). The pin is emsdk 4.0.23 through M2, the toolchain the prebuilt ONNX Runtime WASM archive is built with; the move to 6.x is at the end of M4 (section 11).

Generated artefacts, all checked in `build_web.yml` (`gen.py --check` before the build, the export diff and the node gates after it):

| artefact | source | produced by | checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `web/src/abi/enums.ts` | `abi/anira.yml`, the registry the C headers are generated from: enum values, status codes, defines, `ANIRA_ABI_VERSION`, struct ids | `tools/abi/gen.py` | `gen.py --check` against the committed file (the `anira_abi_generate` CTest and `build_web.yml`) |
| `web/src/abi/bindings.ts` | every function of `abi/anira.yml`: one typed method per entry point over a module instance | `gen.py` | `gen.py --check`; the package's `tsc` build and tests |
| `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt`, `abi/symbols-<major>.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt`, `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` | every function of `abi/anira.yml` (promised, draft, `platform: emscripten`) | `gen.py` | the link (presence); the `AniraWeb.d.ts` export names diffed against `symbols-0.txt` ∪ `symbols-draft.txt` ∪ `symbols-emscripten.txt` ∪ {`malloc`, `free`} in both directions |
| `web/src/abi/layout.ts` | size, align and member offsets of every Tier-1 record | `gen.py`, from the layout model behind `abi/layout-<major>.txt` and `test_layout.c` | `gen.py --check`; gate 3: `test_layout.c`'s printer run under node, its table diffed against `abi/layout-0.txt` |

`validate-options.cmake:116-136` forbids tests on Wasm; the ABI gates are the one explicit exception. The `wasm-abi` preset (wasm-release plus `ANIRA_WITH_ABI_TESTS`) builds them as members of ALL, and `anira_abi_wasm_gates` runs them under node (`test/abi/CMakeLists.txt:384-403`): gate 3's layout printer, its table diffed against `abi/layout-0.txt`, and the smokes `anira_em_smoke`, `anira_em_smoke_thread`, `anira_em_mutex`, `anira_em_pool`, `anira_em_console`, `anira_em_runtime` and `anira_em_oom`, beside gate 4's header compiles. JavaScript never hand-computes a struct offset on the control path and never writes a struct field by hand: every descriptor it must produce has an `anira_em_*_init` or a scalar-setter twin (`anira_em_processor_desc_init` and `anira_em_engine_desc_init` take the flags JavaScript cannot write into the record), and the records it reads (`anira_processor_ctx`, `anira_engine_ctx`, `anira_tensor`, `anira_log_record`, `anira_error`) are read through `layout.ts`. The hand-mirrored constants of 2.x (`get_inference_backend_onnx()`, the libc++ vector layout read in `v2.3.0:web/src/wrappers/InferenceHandler.ts:200-212`) were the drift the ABI exists to end.

Threading model. One module on one shared `WebAssembly.Memory`, linked with `-pthread -sWASM_WORKERS -sAUDIO_WORKLET` (Emscripten's hybrid mode): the page's main thread instantiates it (`Anira.create`), and anira starts every other thread from C on the same memory, each with its own stack and thread-local block. The context's inference threads are Wasm Workers (`InferenceThread::start`, `src/platform/emscripten/em_worker.cpp`), so `num_threads` and `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` mean what they mean natively and `anira_inference_thread_start` starts one for a user-driven thread; a Worker imports the context's worker module (`anira_em_context_config_set_worker_module`) and awaits its registry's `boot` -- the JS engines' `init` -- before it enters `run_loop`, where it stays for its whole life. An idle Worker parks on the core's wake word (`memory.atomic.wait32`, notified at every enqueue and every stop, `src/scheduler/InferenceThread.cpp:58-70`) under either wait strategy; `ANIRA_WAIT_BLOCKING` is coerced to `ANIRA_WAIT_SPIN_BACKOFF` and the log drain to `ANIRA_LOG_DRAIN_MANUAL`, each with a warning (`src/scheduler/Core.cpp:581-608`). Stop protocol: `anira_inference_thread_stop` waits for a started Worker to leave the loop and then terminates it (a Worker still booting has its boot cancelled and is never terminated mid-boot); a loop the host runs itself (`anira_inference_thread_run_loop`) is only asked to return, and `anira_inference_thread_destroy` refuses until `anira_inference_thread_has_exited` (a shared-memory atomic set on `run_loop` exit). A Worker enters its loop only once the main thread has yielded to its event loop, so a main-thread host that waits on an inference first waits, yielding, until `anira_em_inference_threads_in_loop` reaches `anira_num_inference_threads` (`Context.ready()`). The real-time path is a Wasm Audio Worklet node written in C (`anira_em_audio_node_create` / `_prepare` / `_hold` / `_set_static_input` / `_destroy`, `src/platform/emscripten/em_audio.cpp`): per render quantum its callback points planar host tensors at the node's buffers (`anira_tensor_init_host_planar`, no copy), makes one `anira_handler_process_multi` call (`_multi_wait` with a `wait_ms` above 0, an offline render) over every Streamed slot it serves, applies the miss rule in C and serves Static inputs from AudioParams or a seqlock per tensor slot. No code of the module starts a pthread: one started from the browser's main thread runs only once that thread yields, so a join there never returns (`cmake/build-wasm.cmake:33-38`).

Thread-local storage is per thread. Emscripten gives every Wasm Worker and the worklet a stack and a thread-local block of their own; anira allocates an inference Worker's region as its 4 MiB stack plus `__builtin_wasm_tls_size()` (`src/platform/emscripten/em_worker.cpp:173-176`), so a `thread_local` on Wasm is what it is natively. The block the hand-instantiated per-thread modules of PR 9 shared, its 1024-byte budget and the `__builtin_wasm_tls_size() <= 1024` assertion went with them in PR 11.

Waits. With `-pthread` the mutexes are real futex mutexes across the main thread and the Workers (#212, `test/abi/em_mutex.c`), where the no-pthread libraries' were stubs that locked nothing. A `_wait` twin still spins on Wasm ("on WebAssembly every wait spins", `abi/handler.h`), and the audio worklet cannot wait at all (a `std::mutex` there spins), so the C node calls the `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` `anira_handler_process_multi` on a live context and its `_wait` twin only with a `wait_ms` above 0, for an offline render whose host accepts the spin. Allocation across threads is safe -- emmalloc under `SHARED_MEMORY` takes a lock around every operation -- and forbidden on `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` paths and in the worklet by real-time policy, the same policy as native: the render callback allocates nothing and locks nothing; the compiled-in engine's `process` on the Worker allocates as it does natively.

The JS registry. JavaScript cannot supply a C function pointer without `addFunction`, which needs `ALLOW_TABLE_GROWTH` and a table entry in every thread's instance. `anira_em.h`, generated from `abi/anira.yml` like every other header, therefore ships trampolines: C functions compiled into anira that forward through one registry object per JavaScript scope, `globalThis[Symbol.for('anira.registry')]`, which the package's `defineProcessor` and `defineEngine` install (`web/src/registry.ts`).

```c
/* include/anira/anira_em.h -- generated; declarations under __EMSCRIPTEN__ only; installed with anira.h */
#define ANIRA_EM_HOOKS_VERSION 2u                /* the layout of a scope's registry object (its version field); appended to, never reordered */
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_PRE 1u           /* 1u << ANIRA_PHASE_PRE_PROCESS: one index space with anira.hpp's Processor::k_* bits */
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_POST 2u
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_BEFORE 4u
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_AFTER 16u        /* bit 3, ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE, is no processor phase */
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_PREPARE 32u
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_RELEASE 64u
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_RESET 128u
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_UNPREPARE 256u
#define ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_INIT 512u
#define ANIRA_EM_ENGINE_HOOK_RESET 1u            /* process always crosses; reset and query opt in */
#define ANIRA_EM_ENGINE_HOOK_QUERY 2u
#define ANIRA_EM_AUDIO_NODE_FLAG_PARTIAL_STREAMS 1u
anira_status anira_em_processor_desc_init(anira_processor_desc* desc, uint32_t js_key, uint32_t hook_mask, uint32_t processor_flags);   /* one trampoline per hook bit, every other slot NULL, release always set */
anira_status anira_em_pipeline_add_js_processor(anira_pipeline* pipeline, uint32_t js_key, uint32_t hook_mask, uint32_t processor_flags, anira_error* err);
                                                       /* == anira_em_processor_desc_init + anira_pipeline_add_processor: the same registration path as native */
anira_status anira_em_engine_desc_init(anira_engine_desc* desc, uint32_t js_key, uint32_t hook_mask, uint32_t engine_flags);   /* process always, reset and query per bit; init/load/unload/prepare/unprepare NULL */
anira_status anira_em_pipeline_add_js_engine(anira_pipeline* pipeline, const char* engine_id, uint32_t js_key, uint32_t hook_mask, uint32_t engine_flags, anira_error* err);
                                                       /* == desc_init + anira_custom_engine_create(engine_id) + anira_pipeline_add_engine; engine_id is reverse-URI */
void         anira_em_set_log_hook(anira_bool enable); /* the scope registries' log as one more sink beside the C sinks; holds the platform sink (the console by level) off */
anira_status anira_em_context_config_set_worker_module(anira_context_config* config, const char* url, anira_error* err);   /* the one web-only field of a context config */
uint32_t     anira_em_inference_threads_in_loop(void) NB;                                    /* [thread-safe] */
anira_status anira_em_audio_node_create(anira_handler* handler, uint32_t audio_context, const uint32_t* input_slots, uint32_t num_input_slots,
                                        const uint32_t* output_slots, uint32_t num_output_slots, const uint32_t* param_slots, uint32_t num_param_slots,
                                        const uint32_t* tensor_slots, uint32_t num_tensor_slots, uint32_t flags, double wait_ms,
                                        int32_t* out_status, uint32_t* out_node, anira_error* err);   /* asynchronous: out_status reads ANIRA_INCOMPLETE until done */
anira_status anira_em_audio_node_set_static_input(uint32_t node, uint32_t slot, const void* bytes, size_t size, anira_error* err);   /* a seqlock per slot */
anira_status anira_em_audio_node_prepare(uint32_t node, const anira_contract* contract, anira_error* err);   /* the quiescing re-prepare of a running node */
anira_status anira_em_audio_node_hold(uint32_t node, int32_t held, anira_error* err);
void         anira_em_audio_node_destroy(uint32_t node);
/* reserved for post-3.0: anira_em_context_config_set_js_webgpu(mc, js_key, err) -- the browser GPUDevice import, with the asynchronous engine contract below */
/* globalThis[Symbol.for('anira.registry')] = { version, processor(Module, key, phase, a, b), engine(Module, key, phase, a, b, engineIdPtr), log(Module, recordPtr), boot(Module) }
   phase is an anira_phase. A processor: a = ctxPtr, b = preparedPtr in the four data phases and RESET; PREPARE: a = prepareInfoPtr, b = &out_prepared;
   UNPREPARE: a = preparedPtr; INIT: a = initInfoPtr; RELEASE: a = b = 0. An engine: INFERENCE and RESET: a = engineCtxPtr, b = preparedPtr; QUERY: a = initInfoPtr, b = &mask.
   engineIdPtr is the UTF-8 id the engine was created under, so a JS engine can find its own models[] row. */
```

`js_key` is the registry key of the processor or engine: the package's `keyOf` of its name, the 32-bit FNV-1a hash of its UTF-16 code units, the same in every scope, and a registry refuses a second name whose key is taken. The trampolines keep `{js_key, engine_id}` in a small heap record as the descriptor's `user_data` and free it in the descriptor's `release`, which is always set for that purpose. `engine_id` is the second identity and independent of the key: the key finds the JS object, the id finds its `models[]` row, so two JS engines in one Worker read two rows. `hook_mask` decides the slots: an unset bit stays NULL in the descriptor and never crosses into JS, so an unregistered phase costs nothing on its thread, and a NULL `pre_process`/`post_process` runs anira's default body. Each hook fires in the scope whose thread executes the C call -- the worklet for `pre_process`, `post_process` and `reset` under a Hard contract, an inference Worker for `before_inference`, `after_inference` and an engine's `process`, the main thread for `init`, `prepare`, `unprepare`, `release` and `query`. A hook that throws, and a scope without a registry, are caught in the trampoline: the exception is reported on the console (rate-limited, not through the anira log, which may be what threw) and the call returns `ANIRA_ERROR_UNKNOWN` (a processor) or `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` (an engine), which fails the chunk the usual way; no JavaScript exception unwinds through a C frame. A JS engine's C `init`, `load`, `unload`, `prepare` and `unprepare` slots are NULL: it is ready when its Worker's registry has booted, before the Worker enters its loop (`boot` builds every engine the module defined and awaits its `init`). The registry carries `version`, `ANIRA_EM_HOOKS_VERSION`: 2, the processor bits being `1u << ANIRA_PHASE_*`. A per-job JS completion (`anira_em_job_options_set_on_complete_js`) arrives with the Async contract at M3.

Two engine paths behind one ABI. (a) ONNX Runtime Web is a JavaScript engine: `@anira-project/anira/engines/onnxruntime-web` exports the factory `onnxRuntimeWeb(options)`, which the context's worker module defines under `ONNXRUNTIME_WEB_ENGINE_ID` (`defineEngine`), and the pipeline adds it with `addEngine('org.anira.onnxruntime-web')` (`anira_em_pipeline_add_js_engine`); its `models[]` row carries that id. It runs in onnxruntime-web's own WebAssembly memory in each Worker, so the heap-to-heap copy per inference remains (`web/src/engines/onnxruntime-web.ts`), and it reads its entry's model in each Worker (`load(model)`: the bytes `ModelConfig.fromUrl` attached on the main thread, a view of the shared memory, so a model file is fetched once). (b) The compiled-in ORT is `ANIRA_ENGINE_ONNXRUNTIME` on `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU`, its environment on global thread pools of one thread each, the calling one, so it starts no pthread (`src/engines/OnnxRuntimeAdapter.cpp:233-242`, under `USE_ANIRA_WEB`). A JS engine's `process` is synchronous, as in 2.x; asynchronous JS engines are M3's (section 11). Browser WebGPU is not a v3.0.0 deliverable, for schedule and measurement rather than architecture. ORT-Web ships four Wasm artefacts -- plain (CPU only), `.jsep` (TypeScript kernels, Asyncify-linked), `.asyncify` and `.jspi` (both the native Dawn WebGPU EP) -- and in every one capable of WebGPU `_OrtRun` returns a Promise, which settles only when the calling agent returns to its event loop. anira's inference Worker never returns to its loop (it is parked in `run_loop` for the whole session), so the completion cannot be observed *on that thread*; it can be observed on another. Two paths are live and neither touches the header: a **GPU proxy Worker** owning the WebGPU session, rendezvousing with the inference thread through `Atomics.wait` on the shared memory -- legal in a dedicated worker agent, illegal only on the main thread and in worklets -- which fits `anira_engine_desc.process` as tagged (`[inference-thread]`, may block); and a **JSPI-linked module**, which rewrites nothing (the real-time export is byte-identical to the plain build; only the listed async exports are wrapped) but is absent from Safari and contested on Chrome for Android, so it would reach fewer browsers than WebGPU itself. Asyncify is the mechanism that would rewrite the `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` forwarders, and only Asyncify. What is missing is a number, not a design: no admission rule can be written before end-to-end block latency is measured, and the pinned `onnxruntime-*-WASM-static` archive is CPU-only, so this build cannot produce that number today. `ANIRA_PROVIDER_WEBGPU` on `ANIRA_ENGINE_ONNXRUNTIME`, `anira_context_config_set_webgpu` and `anira_tensor_init_wgpu_buffer` stay in the header as the native Dawn path of M4 (`set_webgpu` returns `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` on Emscripten in 3.0); the browser candidate arrives post-3.0 as an explicitly asynchronous JS engine -- an `anira_engine_desc` tail slot `process_async` plus a completion callback, driven by `anira_inference_thread_execute` from an unblocked Worker pump -- or as a JSPI-linked second module variant sharing the header, additive under ABI major 3 (section 10).

Log and version from JS. The C sinks work on Wasm as natively (`anira_context_config_set_log_sink`, a C function compiled into the module); `anira_em_set_log_hook(1)` (`Anira.setLogSink`) adds the scope registries' `log` as one more sink of the module, and while it is registered it holds the platform sink -- on Wasm the console by level, `console.debug`/`.info`/`.warn`/`.error` (`em_console.cpp`) -- off, so a record reaches the console once; without the hook every record goes to the console by level. Every record reaches the registry of the scope that logs or drains it: the main thread for the drained RT records, which the page pumps since the web has no drain thread (`Anira.drainLog`, or `Anira.startLogPump(intervalMs)`, a main-thread `setInterval` over `_anira_drain_log`), and a Worker for the control-path records it logs itself (an ORT error inside `process`), which reach that scope's registry and its `logSink`. TypeScript reads `group` and `message` through `layout.ts` and `UTF8ToString` (`logRecordAt`). `_anira_abi_version()` and `_anira_check_abi(v)` are numbers, `UTF8ToString(_anira_version_string())` is `Anira.version`, and `Anira.create({ abiVersion })` calls `anira_check_abi` and refuses a module that does not serve that ABI.

The TypeScript package `@anira-project/anira` 3.x (`web/src`), ESM, with the entry points `.`, `./scope` (the light entry for an inference Worker's or a worklet's module), `./engines/onnxruntime-web` and `./wasm/*`. Its classes and members are those of `anira.hpp`, in lowerCamelCase, and each class's doc comment names where the web differs:

| file | content |
|---|---|
| `abi/` | `bindings.ts` (one typed method per entry point of the registry over a module instance: strings in and out, out-parameters returned, an `anira_error` thrown as `AniraError`), `enums.ts`, `layout.ts`, `exports_wasm.txt` -- generated by `tools/abi/gen.py`, never edited |
| `Anira.ts` | `Anira.create(options)`: the module on the page's main thread over a shared memory that grows, the main thread's registry booted; `version`, `setLogSink`, `drainLog`, `startLogPump` |
| `Context.ts`, `ModelConfig.ts`, `ContractHandle.ts` | `Context.create(anira, { threads, workerModule })`, `ready()`; `ModelConfig.fromUrl` / `fromJson` (the model file of a native host, each entry's model fetched beside it and attached with `setModelBytes`); `ContractHandle.fromUrl` / `fromJson` / `hard(anira, options)` / `hardHostBlocks(hostBlockMin, hostBlockMax, hostRate)` |
| `Pipeline.ts`, `InferenceHandler.ts`, `PlanReport.ts` | `Pipeline.create(anira).addInference(model).addProcessor(name, options).addEngine(id, options)`; `InferenceHandler.create(context, pipeline)`, `prepare(contract)` returning the `PlanReport`, `getLatency`, `getLatencies`, `setStaticInput`, `setPlan` / `getPlan`, `rtError`, `createAudioNode`; `PlanReport.plans` / `slots` / `extensions` / `find` |
| `AudioNode.ts`, `renderGeometry.ts` | the node of `createAudioNode(audioContext, options)` over `anira_em_audio_node_*`, with `params`, `setStaticInput`, `prepare(contract)` and `hold`; `measureRenderGeometry(audioContext, { hold })` returning `{ quantum, burst }`, the host blocks of a running context |
| `registry.ts`, `scope.ts` | the scope registry: `defineProcessor(name, factory)`, `defineEngine(id, factory)`, `engineTensors`, `engineModel`, `keyOf` |
| `engines/` | `onnxruntime-web.ts`: `onnxRuntimeWeb(options)` and `ONNXRUNTIME_WEB_ENGINE_ID` |

Fate of every 2.x class (`v2.3.0:web/src`):

| 2.x | v3 |
|---|---|
| `VectorSizeT`, `VectorUnsignedInt`, `VectorFloat`, `VectorInt64T`, `VectorVectorInt64`/`TensorShapeList`, `VectorModelData`, `VectorTensorShape`, `VectorRingBuffer`, `VectorBufferF` -- ten classes, 43 `vector_*` exports (48 `EMSCRIPTEN_KEEPALIVE` entries in `src/emscripten-wrappers/utils/Vectors.cpp`, five of them `buffer_f_*`) | deleted; they existed only because the v2 constructors take `std::vector` by reference, and every builder call is one scalar |
| `ModelData`, `TensorShape`, `ProcessingSpec`, `InferenceConfig` | the model file and `ModelConfig.fromUrl` / `fromJson` |
| `HostConfig` | deleted: `ContractHandle.hard(anira, options)` and `.hardHostBlocks(hostBlockMin, hostBlockMax, hostRate)`; the anchor moved to `ModelConfig` (section 5) |
| `BufferF` | the model-end tensors of a processor's and an engine's context (`_anira_processor_input_tensor` / `_output_tensor`, `engineTensors`) |
| `RingBuffer` | `_anira_ring_*` over the ring `_anira_processor_input_ring` / `_output_ring` hands out |
| `PrePostProcessor`, `JSPrePostProcessor` | `defineProcessor(name, factory)` in every scope that runs one of its phases, added with `Pipeline.addProcessor(name, { phases, flags })`; `setInferenceHooks` became the `phases` mask (`ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_*`) |
| `JSBackendBase` | `defineEngine(id, factory)` in the context's worker module, added with `Pipeline.addEngine(id, options)` |
| `createInferenceBackend`, `createFactory`/`Factory<C>` (`v2.3.0:web/src/utils.ts:71-87`), `resolvePtr`/`PossiblePointer` | deleted; engine and provider ids are constants of `enums.ts` and a custom engine is the id it was created under, builders need no arity games |
| `InferenceHandler`, `InferenceThread` | `InferenceHandler` rewritten over the C entries (`InferenceHandler.create(context, pipeline)`), its Hard process, push and pop the audio node's and `getLatencies` reading caller memory; the TS `InferenceThread` deleted: the Workers are anira's, started from C |
| `ONNXRuntimeWebBackend`, `AniraWeb`, `AniraAudioWorkletBase`, `setupInferenceWorker`, `bundleAudioWorklet`, the message protocol, the `performance` polyfill | deleted with the hand-rolled runtime (PR 11): `Anira` instantiates the module once, the C audio node replaces the JS worklet, ONNX Runtime Web is the JS engine `onnxRuntimeWeb()` |

The real-time path is the C audio node: no JavaScript runs per render quantum unless the handler's processor is a JS one. A JS processor's worklet phases call raw exports over numbers -- `_anira_processor_default_pre_process(ctx)`, `_anira_processor_input_tensor(ctx, slot, tensor)` into a record its `prepare` allocated, `_anira_processor_input_ring` / `_output_ring` and `_anira_ring_*` (the batched `_anira_ring_pop_windows` among them), the typed `abi.anira_tensor_data_f32` and `anira_tensor_num_elements` -- and allocate nothing, build no object and no closure (`web/examples/processor-and-engine/gain-processor.ts`): a wrapper object per block is allocation pressure on the render thread.

What the consumer writes. The 2.x web example builds fourteen handles of ten classes to describe one model (`anira-web-example/src/simple-gain-stereo/index.ts:26-61`, on its 2.x `main`):

```ts
const vectorModelData  = aniraWeb.VectorModelData([aniraWeb.ModelData(modelBuffer, aniraWeb.InferenceBackend.ONNX)])
const tensorShape      = aniraWeb.TensorShape(aniraWeb.TensorShapeList([[1, 2, 512], [1]]), aniraWeb.TensorShapeList([[1, 2, 512], [1]]))
const processingSpec   = aniraWeb.ProcessingSpec(aniraWeb.VectorSizeT([2, 1]), aniraWeb.VectorSizeT([2, 1]), aniraWeb.VectorSizeT([512, 0]), aniraWeb.VectorSizeT([512, 0]))
const inferenceConfig  = aniraWeb.InferenceConfig(vectorModelData, aniraWeb.VectorTensorShape([tensorShape]), processingSpec, 5, 10, false, 0, 1)
const hostAudioConfig  = aniraWeb.HostConfig(128, 48000, false, 0)
```

In v3 the model and contract files of a native host load unchanged, the render geometry is measured on the running context, and the handler streams through its audio node (`web/README.md:37-56`):

```ts
const audioContext = new AudioContext({ sampleRate: 48000 })
const anira = await Anira.create()
anira.setLogSink()
anira.startLogPump()

const context = Context.create(anira) // a pool of Wasm Workers, half the cores
// The same files a native host loads; the ONNX entry's model file is fetched beside the config.
const model = await ModelConfig.fromUrl(anira, '/models/gain.model.json')
await audioContext.resume() // the geometry is measured on a running context
const geometry = await measureRenderGeometry(audioContext) // the render quantum, the burst
const contract = (
  await ContractHandle.fromUrl(anira, '/models/gain.contract.json')
).hardHostBlocks(geometry.quantum, geometry.burst, audioContext.sampleRate)
const handler = InferenceHandler.create(context, Pipeline.create(anira).addInference(model))
const report = handler.prepare(contract) // the plans, as anira::InferenceHandler::prepare
console.log(`${report.numPlans()} plan(s), latency ${handler.getLatency()} samples`)

const audioNode = await handler.createAudioNode(audioContext, { params: [1] })
audioNode.params.get(1)!.value = 0.5 // the model's Static gain
source.connect(audioNode.node).connect(audioContext.destination)
```

---

## 7. Planner, stages, chunkers
